I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork, like I missed some payments and that’s the whole story, but it isn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, even some people at my job—I know they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house.

And, you know, I’m a supervisor, I notice patterns. I deal with schedules and numbers and people saying one thing but meaning another all day. So when they hand me papers and say, “See, this explains it,” it doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It’s not just forms. There’s something coordinated going on.

Other situations… I mean, it mostly comes back to the house. That’s the thing people keep trying to make sound simple, like, “You missed payments, here are the papers,” and then I’m supposed to just accept that.

But it’s not just the bank by itself. It’s the loan servicer too, and then even at work there are these little things—comments, timing, people acting like they know something before I’ve said anything. I’m a supervisor, so I pay attention to that kind of thing. Schedules, who’s talking to who, when things happen. And when it lines up for almost two years, it’s hard not to see that they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house.

So yes, I guess that’s the situation. It’s not “other” exactly—it’s all connected. No amount of paperwork they put in front of me changes what I’ve been seeing.

I mean… nothing has nothing to do with it, you know? That’s what people don’t understand. They want me to separate everything into neat little boxes—house over here, job over there, bank paperwork somewhere else—but that’s not how it’s been happening.

It started with the foreclosure stuff, or at least that’s where I really noticed it. Everyone keeps saying it’s just missed payments and forms, but the bank says one thing, the loan servicer does another, and then people at work start acting like they already know. So when you ask if it’s outside the house or job… I don’t know, because it all circles back to that.

I’m not saying every person on the street is against me. But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.

It feels like they’re all quietly helping push me out of my house.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other situations where you felt like people or organizations were working against you?\n\nPatient: Other situations… I mean, it mostly comes back to the house. That’s the thing people keep trying to make sound simple, like, “You missed payments, here are the papers,” and then I’m supposed to just accept that.\n\nBut it’s not just the bank by itself. It’s the loan servicer too, and then even at work there are these little things—comments, timing, people acting like they know something before I’ve said anything. I’m a supervisor, so I pay attention to that kind of thing. Schedules, who’s talking to who, when things happen. And when it lines up for almost two years, it’s hard not to see that they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nSo yes, I guess that’s the situation. It’s not “other” exactly—it’s all connected. No amount of paperwork they put in front of me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are working against you in situations that have nothing to do with your house or job?\n\nPatient: I mean… nothing has nothing to do with it, you know? That’s what people don’t understand. They want me to separate everything into neat little boxes—house over here, job over there, bank paperwork somewhere else—but that’s not how it’s been happening.\n\nIt started with the foreclosure stuff, or at least that’s where I really noticed it. Everyone keeps saying it’s just missed payments and forms, but the bank says one thing, the loan servicer does another, and then people at work start acting like they already know. So when you ask if it’s outside the house or job… I don’t know, because it all circles back to that.\n\nI’m not saying every person on the street is against me. But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Changed? Not really. I mean, at first I tried to tell myself maybe it was just the bank being disorganized, because banks do that, and I’ve worked retail long enough to know people mess up paperwork all the time. The point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes that. It’s been almost two years of patterns, not just one mistake.

But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you ever changed since you first started noticing these patterns?\n\nPatient: Changed? Not really. I mean, at first I tried to tell myself maybe it was just the bank being disorganized, because banks do that, and I’ve worked retail long enough to know people mess up paperwork all the time. Like when inventory counts are off and everybody swears they checked the back room, but then you find a whole box behind seasonal displays—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes that. It’s been almost two years of patterns, not just one mistake.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’ve considered it because people keep asking me to, you know? Like, “Could you be mistaken?” “Could it just be paperwork?” And I try to be reasonable—I supervise people, I know mistakes happen. Someone misfiles something, someone forgets to call back, the schedule gets changed and nobody tells you… retail is full of that kind of thing.

But this isn’t one mistake. That’s what I keep trying to explain. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and papers, like if they stack enough documents in front of me I’ll stop noticing what’s been going on. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing it for almost two years.

So, mistaken? I mean… I’ve asked myself that, sure. But no amount of paperwork changes what I’ve seen.

I am still working, but people are more careful around me now.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 9 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 9 months, since the foreclosure notices and bank calls started taking over our home life.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are working against you in situations that have nothing to do with your house or job?\n\nPatient: I mean… nothing has nothing to do with it, you know? That’s what people don’t understand. They want me to separate everything into neat little boxes—house over here, job over there, bank paperwork somewhere else—but that’s not how it’s been happening.\n\nIt started with the foreclosure stuff, or at least that’s where I really noticed it. Everyone keeps saying it’s just missed payments and forms, but the bank says one thing, the loan servicer does another, and then people at work start acting like they already know. So when you ask if it’s outside the house or job… I don’t know, because it all circles back to that.\n\nI’m not saying every person on the street is against me. But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you ever changed since you first started noticing these patterns?\n\nPatient: Changed? Not really. I mean, at first I tried to tell myself maybe it was just the bank being disorganized, because banks do that, and I’ve worked retail long enough to know people mess up paperwork all the time. Like when inventory counts are off and everybody swears they checked the back room, but then you find a whole box behind seasonal displays—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes that. It’s been almost two years of patterns, not just one mistake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because people keep asking me to, you know? Like, “Could you be mistaken?” “Could it just be paperwork?” And I try to be reasonable—I supervise people, I know mistakes happen. Someone misfiles something, someone forgets to call back, the schedule gets changed and nobody tells you… retail is full of that kind of thing.\n\nBut this isn’t one mistake. That’s what I keep trying to explain. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and papers, like if they stack enough documents in front of me I’ll stop noticing what’s been going on. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing it for almost two years.\n\nSo, mistaken? I mean… I’ve asked myself that, sure. Doubts… I mean, people keep wanting me to call them doubts. Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.

I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know what I’ve been seeing. Like when inventory counts are off and everybody swears they checked the back room, but then you find a whole box behind seasonal displays—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. Like, “Could you be mistaken?” “Could it just be paperwork?” And I try to be reasonable—I supervise people, I know mistakes happen. That’s what I keep trying to explain. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and papers, like if they stack enough documents in front of me I’ll stop noticing what’s been going on. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing it for almost two years.\n\nSo, mistaken? I mean… I’ve asked myself that, sure. But no amount of paperwork changes what I’ve seen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working against you?\n\nPatient: Doubts… I mean, people keep wanting me to call them doubts. Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head plenty of times, especially when I’m trying to sleep, or when I’m at work and someone says something just a little too specific—sorry, that probably sounds like I’m jumping around. But no, not real doubts. Not the kind where I think, “Oh, maybe this is all nothing.”\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know what I’ve been seeing. Yes. I mean, not like I think everybody is sitting around all day talking about me, people have their own lives, schedules, whatever—at work half the time they’re talking about coupons or who didn’t cover register three.

Everyone keeps trying to make it sound like this is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they say that enough times it becomes true. But the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing the pattern for almost two years now.

And then someone at work will say something small, like not even directly about me, but close enough that I know they’ve heard things. No amount of papers they show me changes that.

The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing it for almost two years.\n\nSo, mistaken? I mean… I’ve asked myself that, sure. But no amount of paperwork changes what I’ve seen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working against you?\n\nPatient: Doubts… I mean, people keep wanting me to call them doubts. Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head plenty of times, especially when I’m trying to sleep, or when I’m at work and someone says something just a little too specific—sorry, that probably sounds like I’m jumping around. But no, not real doubts. Not the kind where I think, “Oh, maybe this is all nothing.”\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes almost two years of that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are talking about you or your situation when you're not around?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, not like I think everybody is sitting around all day talking about me, people have their own lives, schedules, whatever—at work half the time they’re talking about coupons or who didn’t cover register three.\n\nBut yes, I do think people talk when I’m not around. About the house, the foreclosure, what they think I don’t notice. That’s part of what’s been going on. Everyone keeps trying to make it sound like this is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they say that enough times it becomes true. But the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing the pattern for almost two years now.\n\nAnd then someone at work will say something small, like not even directly about me, but close enough that I know they’ve heard things. No amount of papers they show me changes that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know… I mean, not “voices” like in a movie or something, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes houses make sounds, you know? I am still working, but people are more careful around me now.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 9 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 9 months, since the foreclosure notices and bank calls started taking over our home life.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working against you?\n\nPatient: Doubts… I mean, people keep wanting me to call them doubts. Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head plenty of times, especially when I’m trying to sleep, or when I’m at work and someone says something just a little too specific—sorry, that probably sounds like I’m jumping around. But no, not real doubts. Not the kind where I think, “Oh, maybe this is all nothing.”\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes almost two years of that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are talking about you or your situation when you're not around?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, not like I think everybody is sitting around all day talking about me, people have their own lives, schedules, whatever—at work half the time they’re talking about coupons or who didn’t cover register three.\n\nBut yes, I do think people talk when I’m not around. About the house, the foreclosure, what they think I don’t notice. That’s part of what’s been going on. Everyone keeps trying to make it sound like this is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they say that enough times it becomes true. But the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing the pattern for almost two years now.\n\nAnd then someone at work will say something small, like not even directly about me, but close enough that I know they’ve heard things. No amount of papers they show me changes that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not “voices” like in a movie or something, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes houses make sounds, you know? Pipes, neighbors, cars outside—my place has this one vent that clicks at night and it drives me crazy because then you’re lying there thinking about everything you didn’t get done that day.\n\nBut other people not hearing it… I don’t know. I haven’t really gone around asking people, “Did you hear that?” Usually I’m alone when I notice noises, or I’m at work and there’s always noise—registers, scanners, somebody dropping a box in the stockroom. So… I’m not sure what exactly counts.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking about your situation?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I mean, it depends what you call “one topic,” because with this whole situation everything connects, or at least it feels like it does when I’m trying to explain it.

Like, if I start with the bank, then I have to explain the loan servicer, and then something that happened at work comes into it, and then someone says I’m “getting off track.” But I’m not trying to ramble. I’m trying to make sure you understand the whole picture, because if I leave out one part, people act like it doesn’t matter.

So… difficult? Maybe. I don’t really notice it unless someone points it out.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork, like I missed some payments and that’s the whole story, but it isn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, even some people at my job—I know they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nAnd, you know, I’m a supervisor, I notice patterns. I deal with schedules and numbers and people saying one thing but meaning another all day. So when they hand me papers and say, “See, this explains it,” it doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It’s not just forms. There’s something coordinated going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, I don’t know if they all sit in one room together or anything like that, but yes, I believe they’re connected. People keep saying it’s just missed payments and foreclosure paperwork, but I’ve watched this for almost two years.\n\nThe bank says one thing, the loan servicer sends something else, then someone at work suddenly starts acting strange around scheduling or making comments like they know something. And I’m a supervisor, I notice when things line up that shouldn’t. I do it at work all the time—patterns, timing, who talks to who. So when they show me papers, it doesn’t undo what I’ve been seeing. It feels like they’re all quietly helping push me out of my house.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other situations where you felt like people or organizations were working against you?\n\nPatient: Other situations… I mean, it mostly comes back to the house. That’s the thing people keep trying to make sound simple, like, “You missed payments, here are the papers,” and then I’m supposed to just accept that.\n\nBut it’s not just the bank by itself. It’s the loan servicer too, and then even at work there are these little things—comments, timing, people acting like they know something before I’ve said anything. I’m a supervisor, so I pay attention to that kind of thing. Schedules, who’s talking to who, when things happen. And when it lines up for almost two years, it’s hard not to see that they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nSo yes, I guess that’s the situation. It’s not “other” exactly—it’s all connected. No amount of paperwork they put in front of me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are working against you in situations that have nothing to do with your house or job?\n\nPatient: I mean… nothing has nothing to do with it, you know? That’s what people don’t understand. They want me to separate everything into neat little boxes—house over here, job over there, bank paperwork somewhere else—but that’s not how it’s been happening.\n\nIt started with the foreclosure stuff, or at least that’s where I really noticed it. Everyone keeps saying it’s just missed payments and forms, but the bank says one thing, the loan servicer does another, and then people at work start acting like they already know. So when you ask if it’s outside the house or job… I don’t know, because it all circles back to that.\n\nI’m not saying every person on the street is against me. But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you ever changed since you first started noticing these patterns?\n\nPatient: Changed? Not really. I mean, at first I tried to tell myself maybe it was just the bank being disorganized, because banks do that, and I’ve worked retail long enough to know people mess up paperwork all the time. Like when inventory counts are off and everybody swears they checked the back room, but then you find a whole box behind seasonal displays—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes that. It’s been almost two years of patterns, not just one mistake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because people keep asking me to, you know? Like, “Could you be mistaken?” “Could it just be paperwork?” And I try to be reasonable—I supervise people, I know mistakes happen. That’s what I keep trying to explain. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and papers, like if they stack enough documents in front of me I’ll stop noticing what’s been going on. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing it for almost two years.\n\nSo, mistaken? I mean… I’ve asked myself that, sure. But no amount of paperwork changes what I’ve seen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working against you?\n\nPatient: Doubts… I mean, people keep wanting me to call them doubts. Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head plenty of times, especially when I’m trying to sleep, or when I’m at work and someone says something just a little too specific—sorry, that probably sounds like I’m jumping around. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork, like I missed some payments and that’s the whole story, but it isn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, even some people at my job—I know they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nAnd, you know, I’m a supervisor, I notice patterns. I deal with schedules and numbers and people saying one thing but meaning another all day. So when they hand me papers and say, “See, this explains it,” it doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It’s not just forms. There’s something coordinated going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, I don’t know if they all sit in one room together or anything like that, but yes, I believe they’re connected. People keep saying it’s just missed payments and foreclosure paperwork, but I’ve watched this for almost two years.\n\nThe bank says one thing, the loan servicer sends something else, then someone at work suddenly starts acting strange around scheduling or making comments like they know something. And I’m a supervisor, I notice when things line up that shouldn’t. I do it at work all the time—patterns, timing, who talks to who. So when they show me papers, it doesn’t undo what I’ve been seeing. It feels like they’re all quietly helping push me out of my house.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other situations where you felt like people or organizations were working against you?\n\nPatient: Other situations… I mean, it mostly comes back to the house. That’s the thing people keep trying to make sound simple, like, “You missed payments, here are the papers,” and then I’m supposed to just accept that.\n\nBut it’s not just the bank by itself. It’s the loan servicer too, and then even at work there are these little things—comments, timing, people acting like they know something before I’ve said anything. I’m a supervisor, so I pay attention to that kind of thing. Schedules, who’s talking to who, when things happen. And when it lines up for almost two years, it’s hard not to see that they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nSo yes, I guess that’s the situation. It’s not “other” exactly—it’s all connected. No amount of paperwork they put in front of me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are working against you in situations that have nothing to do with your house or job?\n\nPatient: I mean… nothing has nothing to do with it, you know? That’s what people don’t understand. They want me to separate everything into neat little boxes—house over here, job over there, bank paperwork somewhere else—but that’s not how it’s been happening.\n\nIt started with the foreclosure stuff, or at least that’s where I really noticed it. Everyone keeps saying it’s just missed payments and forms, but the bank says one thing, the loan servicer does another, and then people at work start acting like they already know. So when you ask if it’s outside the house or job… I don’t know, because it all circles back to that.\n\nI’m not saying every person on the street is against me. But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you ever changed since you first started noticing these patterns?\n\nPatient: Changed? Not really. I mean, at first I tried to tell myself maybe it was just the bank being disorganized, because banks do that, and I’ve worked retail long enough to know people mess up paperwork all the time. Like when inventory counts are off and everybody swears they checked the back room, but then you find a whole box behind seasonal displays—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes that. It’s been almost two years of patterns, not just one mistake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because people keep asking me to, you know? Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head plenty of times, especially when I’m trying to sleep, or when I’m at work and someone says something just a little too specific—sorry, that probably sounds like I’m jumping around. No amount of papers they show me changes that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not “voices” like in a movie or something, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes houses make sounds, you know? Pipes, neighbors, cars outside—my place has this one vent that clicks at night and it drives me crazy because then you’re lying there thinking about everything you didn’t get done that day.\n\nBut other people not hearing it… I don’t know. I haven’t really gone around asking people, “Did you hear that?” Usually I’m alone when I notice noises, or I’m at work and there’s always noise—registers, scanners, somebody dropping a box in the stockroom. So… I’m not sure what exactly counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking about your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I mean, it depends what you call “one topic,” because with this whole situation everything connects, or at least it feels like it does when I’m trying to explain it.\n\nLike, if I start with the bank, then I have to explain the loan servicer, and then something that happened at work comes into it, and then someone says I’m “getting off track.” But I’m not trying to ramble. I’m trying to make sure you understand the whole picture, because if I leave out one part, people act like it doesn’t matter.\n\nSo… difficult? Maybe. I don’t really notice it unless someone points it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant stressors or life changes in the past two years, unrelated to your job or the house?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… unrelated to the job or the house is hard, because those two things sort of bled into everything else. Like, even going to the grocery store became about counting every dollar, and then you run into someone from work in the cereal aisle and suddenly it doesn’t feel separate anymore.\n\nBut if you mean like a death in the family, divorce, big move, something like that… no, I don’t think so. Nothing major that I can point to and say, “That’s the thing.” It’s mostly been… all of this. Not like… the TV is sending me messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking the anchor is talking directly to me.

But sometimes I’ll see a story about foreclosures or banks getting fined, and it’s hard not to think, well, exactly, that’s the kind of thing I’ve been saying. Everyone keeps treating my foreclosure like it’s just paperwork and missed payments, but I know it’s not just that. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house.

And then when I try to explain it, people want to show me forms and dates and balances like that cancels out what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It doesn’t. Papers can be made to look clean. That doesn’t mean there isn’t something going on underneath.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork, like I missed some payments and that’s the whole story, but it isn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, even some people at my job—I know they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nAnd, you know, I’m a supervisor, I notice patterns. I deal with schedules and numbers and people saying one thing but meaning another all day. So when they hand me papers and say, “See, this explains it,” it doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It’s not just forms. There’s something coordinated going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, I don’t know if they all sit in one room together or anything like that, but yes, I believe they’re connected. People keep saying it’s just missed payments and foreclosure paperwork, but I’ve watched this for almost two years.\n\nThe bank says one thing, the loan servicer sends something else, then someone at work suddenly starts acting strange around scheduling or making comments like they know something. And I’m a supervisor, I notice when things line up that shouldn’t. I do it at work all the time—patterns, timing, who talks to who. So when they show me papers, it doesn’t undo what I’ve been seeing. It feels like they’re all quietly helping push me out of my house.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other situations where you felt like people or organizations were working against you?\n\nPatient: Other situations… I mean, it mostly comes back to the house. No amount of paperwork they put in front of me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are working against you in situations that have nothing to do with your house or job?\n\nPatient: I mean… nothing has nothing to do with it, you know? That’s what people don’t understand. They want me to separate everything into neat little boxes—house over here, job over there, bank paperwork somewhere else—but that’s not how it’s been happening.\n\nIt started with the foreclosure stuff, or at least that’s where I really noticed it. Everyone keeps saying it’s just missed payments and forms, but the bank says one thing, the loan servicer does another, and then people at work start acting like they already know. So when you ask if it’s outside the house or job… I don’t know, because it all circles back to that.\n\nI’m not saying every person on the street is against me. But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you ever changed since you first started noticing these patterns?\n\nPatient: Changed? Not really. I mean, at first I tried to tell myself maybe it was just the bank being disorganized, because banks do that, and I’ve worked retail long enough to know people mess up paperwork all the time. Like when inventory counts are off and everybody swears they checked the back room, but then you find a whole box behind seasonal displays—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes that. It’s been almost two years of patterns, not just one mistake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because people keep asking me to, you know? Like, “Could you be mistaken?” “Could it just be paperwork?” And I try to be reasonable—I supervise people, I know mistakes happen. Someone misfiles something, someone forgets to call back, the schedule gets changed and nobody tells you… retail is full of that kind of thing.\n\nBut this isn’t one mistake. That’s what I keep trying to explain. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and papers, like if they stack enough documents in front of me I’ll stop noticing what’s been going on. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing it for almost two years.\n\nSo, mistaken? I mean… I’ve asked myself that, sure. But no amount of paperwork changes what I’ve seen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working against you?\n\nPatient: Doubts… I mean, people keep wanting me to call them doubts. Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head plenty of times, especially when I’m trying to sleep, or when I’m at work and someone says something just a little too specific—sorry, that probably sounds like I’m jumping around. But no, not real doubts. Not the kind where I think, “Oh, maybe this is all nothing.”\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes almost two years of that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are talking about you or your situation when you're not around?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, not like I think everybody is sitting around all day talking about me, people have their own lives, schedules, whatever—at work half the time they’re talking about coupons or who didn’t cover register three.\n\nBut yes, I do think people talk when I’m not around. About the house, the foreclosure, what they think I don’t notice. That’s part of what’s been going on. Everyone keeps trying to make it sound like this is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they say that enough times it becomes true. But the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing the pattern for almost two years now.\n\nAnd then someone at work will say something small, like not even directly about me, but close enough that I know they’ve heard things. No amount of papers they show me changes that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not “voices” like in a movie or something, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes houses make sounds, you know? Pipes, neighbors, cars outside—my place has this one vent that clicks at night and it drives me crazy because then you’re lying there thinking about everything you didn’t get done that day.\n\nBut other people not hearing it… I don’t know. I haven’t really gone around asking people, “Did you hear that?” Usually I’m alone when I notice noises, or I’m at work and there’s always noise—registers, scanners, somebody dropping a box in the stockroom. So… I’m not sure what exactly counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking about your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I mean, it depends what you call “one topic,” because with this whole situation everything connects, or at least it feels like it does when I’m trying to explain it.\n\nLike, if I start with the bank, then I have to explain the loan servicer, and then something that happened at work comes into it, and then someone says I’m “getting off track.” But I’m not trying to ramble. I’m trying to make sure you understand the whole picture, because if I leave out one part, people act like it doesn’t matter.\n\nSo… difficult? Maybe. I don’t really notice it unless someone points it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant stressors or life changes in the past two years, unrelated to your job or the house?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… unrelated to the job or the house is hard, because those two things sort of bled into everything else. Like, even going to the grocery store became about counting every dollar, and then you run into someone from work in the cereal aisle and suddenly it doesn’t feel separate anymore.\n\nBut if you mean like a death in the family, divorce, big move, something like that… no, I don’t think so. Nothing major that I can point to and say, “That’s the thing.” It’s mostly been… all of this. And then all the little things around it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things you see on TV, or read in the newspaper, are somehow related to you?\n\nPatient: Not like… the TV is sending me messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking the anchor is talking directly to me.\n\nBut sometimes I’ll see a story about foreclosures or banks getting fined, and it’s hard not to think, well, exactly, that’s the kind of thing I’ve been saying. Everyone keeps treating my foreclosure like it’s just paperwork and missed payments, but I know it’s not just that. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nAnd then when I try to explain it, people want to show me forms and dates and balances like that cancels out what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It doesn’t. Papers can be made to look clean. That doesn’t mean there isn’t something going on underneath.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Before the house? I mean… I wouldn’t say like this, no. Not in the same way.

I mean… I guess if there were real new information, sure, anyone can change their mind. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. But that’s the thing—what they keep calling “new information” is always just more paperwork, more statements, more dates and balances like that explains everything.

And paperwork doesn’t erase what I’ve been seeing. I’ve been dealing with this for almost two years, and it’s not just the bank in some office somewhere. It’s the loan servicer, and then people at my job acting like they know things, little comments, shifts changing, the way certain things lined up… and then I’m supposed to believe it’s all separate? It reminds me of when customers would come in with receipts and swear the sale sign was different, and sometimes the receipt doesn’t tell the whole story, you know?

So, could something change my understanding? Maybe in theory. But not just another clean-looking form shoved in front of me. Because I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, and I know they’ve been working together to push me out of my house.

It’s the loan servicer too, and then even at work there are these little things—comments, timing, people acting like they know something before I’ve said anything. I’m a supervisor, so I pay attention to that kind of thing. Schedules, who’s talking to who, when things happen. And when it lines up for almost two years, it’s hard not to see that they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nSo yes, I guess that’s the situation. It’s not “other” exactly—it’s all connected. No amount of paperwork they put in front of me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are working against you in situations that have nothing to do with your house or job?\n\nPatient: I mean… nothing has nothing to do with it, you know? That’s what people don’t understand. They want me to separate everything into neat little boxes—house over here, job over there, bank paperwork somewhere else—but that’s not how it’s been happening.\n\nIt started with the foreclosure stuff, or at least that’s where I really noticed it. Everyone keeps saying it’s just missed payments and forms, but the bank says one thing, the loan servicer does another, and then people at work start acting like they already know. So when you ask if it’s outside the house or job… I don’t know, because it all circles back to that.\n\nI’m not saying every person on the street is against me. But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you ever changed since you first started noticing these patterns?\n\nPatient: Changed? Not really. I mean, at first I tried to tell myself maybe it was just the bank being disorganized, because banks do that, and I’ve worked retail long enough to know people mess up paperwork all the time. Like when inventory counts are off and everybody swears they checked the back room, but then you find a whole box behind seasonal displays—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes that. It’s been almost two years of patterns, not just one mistake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because people keep asking me to, you know? Like, “Could you be mistaken?” “Could it just be paperwork?” And I try to be reasonable—I supervise people, I know mistakes happen. Someone misfiles something, someone forgets to call back, the schedule gets changed and nobody tells you… retail is full of that kind of thing.\n\nBut this isn’t one mistake. That’s what I keep trying to explain. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and papers, like if they stack enough documents in front of me I’ll stop noticing what’s been going on. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing it for almost two years.\n\nSo, mistaken? I mean… I’ve asked myself that, sure. But no amount of paperwork changes what I’ve seen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working against you?\n\nPatient: Doubts… I mean, people keep wanting me to call them doubts. Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head plenty of times, especially when I’m trying to sleep, or when I’m at work and someone says something just a little too specific—sorry, that probably sounds like I’m jumping around. But no, not real doubts. Not the kind where I think, “Oh, maybe this is all nothing.”\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes almost two years of that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are talking about you or your situation when you're not around?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, not like I think everybody is sitting around all day talking about me, people have their own lives, schedules, whatever—at work half the time they’re talking about coupons or who didn’t cover register three.\n\nBut yes, I do think people talk when I’m not around. About the house, the foreclosure, what they think I don’t notice. That’s part of what’s been going on. Everyone keeps trying to make it sound like this is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they say that enough times it becomes true. But the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing the pattern for almost two years now.\n\nAnd then someone at work will say something small, like not even directly about me, but close enough that I know they’ve heard things. No amount of papers they show me changes that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not “voices” like in a movie or something, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes houses make sounds, you know? Pipes, neighbors, cars outside—my place has this one vent that clicks at night and it drives me crazy because then you’re lying there thinking about everything you didn’t get done that day.\n\nBut other people not hearing it… I don’t know. I haven’t really gone around asking people, “Did you hear that?” Usually I’m alone when I notice noises, or I’m at work and there’s always noise—registers, scanners, somebody dropping a box in the stockroom. So… I’m not sure what exactly counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking about your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I mean, it depends what you call “one topic,” because with this whole situation everything connects, or at least it feels like it does when I’m trying to explain it.\n\nLike, if I start with the bank, then I have to explain the loan servicer, and then something that happened at work comes into it, and then someone says I’m “getting off track.” But I’m not trying to ramble. I’m trying to make sure you understand the whole picture, because if I leave out one part, people act like it doesn’t matter.\n\nSo… difficult? Maybe. I don’t really notice it unless someone points it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant stressors or life changes in the past two years, unrelated to your job or the house?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… unrelated to the job or the house is hard, because those two things sort of bled into everything else. Like, even going to the grocery store became about counting every dollar, and then you run into someone from work in the cereal aisle and suddenly it doesn’t feel separate anymore.\n\nBut if you mean like a death in the family, divorce, big move, something like that… no, I don’t think so. Nothing major that I can point to and say, “That’s the thing.” It’s mostly been… all of this. And then all the little things around it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things you see on TV, or read in the newspaper, are somehow related to you?\n\nPatient: Not like… the TV is sending me messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking the anchor is talking directly to me.\n\nBut sometimes I’ll see a story about foreclosures or banks getting fined, and it’s hard not to think, well, exactly, that’s the kind of thing I’ve been saying. Everyone keeps treating my foreclosure like it’s just paperwork and missed payments, but I know it’s not just that. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nAnd then when I try to explain it, people want to show me forms and dates and balances like that cancels out what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It doesn’t. Papers can be made to look clean. That doesn’t mean there isn’t something going on underneath.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had similar thoughts or beliefs about other people or organizations working against you before this situation with your house?\n\nPatient: Before the house? I mean… I wouldn’t say like this, no. Not in the same way.\n\nPeople act like I’ve always been suspicious or something, but this started because of what actually happened. The foreclosure, the bank, the loan servicer… and then things at work started lining up too neatly, like people there knew things they shouldn’t have known. So now everyone says it’s “just paperwork” or “missed payments,” but that’s not what I’ve been seeing.\n\nI guess I’ve had times where I thought someone at work didn’t like me, or a manager was being unfair, but that’s normal, isn’t it? This is different. This has been almost two years of them quietly working together to push me out of my house, and showing me papers doesn’t make that go away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that new information could change your understanding of the situation with the bank, loan servicer, and your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess if there were real new information, sure, anyone can change their mind. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. But that’s the thing—what they keep calling “new information” is always just more paperwork, more statements, more dates and balances like that explains everything.\n\nAnd paperwork doesn’t erase what I’ve been seeing. I’ve been dealing with this for almost two years, and it’s not just the bank in some office somewhere. It’s the loan servicer, and then people at my job acting like they know things, little comments, shifts changing, the way certain things lined up… and then I’m supposed to believe it’s all separate? It reminds me of when customers would come in with receipts and swear the sale sign was different, and sometimes the receipt doesn’t tell the whole story, you know?\n\nSo, could something change my understanding? Maybe in theory. But not just another clean-looking form shoved in front of me. Because I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, and I know they’ve been working together to push me out of my house.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It doesn’t. Papers can be made to look clean. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Before the house thing? Not really… not like this.

I mean, at work you always have people who don’t like you, or a manager who makes things harder than they need to be. Retail is like that—people smile at you one minute and then schedule you for the worst shifts the next, and customers think you personally control the whole store policy. But that’s just work stuff.

This is different. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know it’s not. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And they keep showing me papers like that’s supposed to settle it, but papers don’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork, like I missed some payments and that’s the whole story, but it isn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, even some people at my job—I know they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nAnd, you know, I’m a supervisor, I notice patterns. I deal with schedules and numbers and people saying one thing but meaning another all day. So when they hand me papers and say, “See, this explains it,” it doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It’s not just forms. There’s something coordinated going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, I don’t know if they all sit in one room together or anything like that, but yes, I believe they’re connected. People keep saying it’s just missed payments and foreclosure paperwork, but I’ve watched this for almost two years.\n\nThe bank says one thing, the loan servicer sends something else, then someone at work suddenly starts acting strange around scheduling or making comments like they know something. And I’m a supervisor, I notice when things line up that shouldn’t. I do it at work all the time—patterns, timing, who talks to who. So when they show me papers, it doesn’t undo what I’ve been seeing. It feels like they’re all quietly helping push me out of my house.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other situations where you felt like people or organizations were working against you?\n\nPatient: Other situations… I mean, it mostly comes back to the house. That’s the thing people keep trying to make sound simple, like, “You missed payments, here are the papers,” and then I’m supposed to just accept that.\n\nBut it’s not just the bank by itself. It’s the loan servicer too, and then even at work there are these little things—comments, timing, people acting like they know something before I’ve said anything. I’m a supervisor, so I pay attention to that kind of thing. Schedules, who’s talking to who, when things happen. And when it lines up for almost two years, it’s hard not to see that they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nSo yes, I guess that’s the situation. It’s not “other” exactly—it’s all connected. No amount of paperwork they put in front of me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are working against you in situations that have nothing to do with your house or job?\n\nPatient: I mean… nothing has nothing to do with it, you know? That’s what people don’t understand. They want me to separate everything into neat little boxes—house over here, job over there, bank paperwork somewhere else—but that’s not how it’s been happening.\n\nIt started with the foreclosure stuff, or at least that’s where I really noticed it. Everyone keeps saying it’s just missed payments and forms, but the bank says one thing, the loan servicer does another, and then people at work start acting like they already know. So when you ask if it’s outside the house or job… I don’t know, because it all circles back to that.\n\nI’m not saying every person on the street is against me. But the bank, the servicer, and some people at my job—yes, I think they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And after almost two years of seeing the same patterns, papers don’t just erase that.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you ever changed since you first started noticing these patterns?\n\nPatient: Changed? Not really. I mean, at first I tried to tell myself maybe it was just the bank being disorganized, because banks do that, and I’ve worked retail long enough to know people mess up paperwork all the time. Like when inventory counts are off and everybody swears they checked the back room, but then you find a whole box behind seasonal displays—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, the more it went on, the clearer it got. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes that. It’s been almost two years of patterns, not just one mistake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job might be mistaken?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because people keep asking me to, you know? Like, “Could you be mistaken?” “Could it just be paperwork?” And I try to be reasonable—I supervise people, I know mistakes happen. Someone misfiles something, someone forgets to call back, the schedule gets changed and nobody tells you… retail is full of that kind of thing.\n\nBut this isn’t one mistake. That’s what I keep trying to explain. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and papers, like if they stack enough documents in front of me I’ll stop noticing what’s been going on. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing it for almost two years.\n\nSo, mistaken? I mean… I’ve asked myself that, sure. But no amount of paperwork changes what I’ve seen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working against you?\n\nPatient: Doubts… I mean, people keep wanting me to call them doubts. Like if I say I wondered about it once, then suddenly the whole thing gets written off as stress or confusion or whatever.\n\nI’ve gone over it in my head plenty of times, especially when I’m trying to sleep, or when I’m at work and someone says something just a little too specific—sorry, that probably sounds like I’m jumping around. But no, not real doubts. Not the kind where I think, “Oh, maybe this is all nothing.”\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes almost two years of that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are talking about you or your situation when you're not around?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, not like I think everybody is sitting around all day talking about me, people have their own lives, schedules, whatever—at work half the time they’re talking about coupons or who didn’t cover register three.\n\nBut yes, I do think people talk when I’m not around. About the house, the foreclosure, what they think I don’t notice. That’s part of what’s been going on. Everyone keeps trying to make it sound like this is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they say that enough times it becomes true. But the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing the pattern for almost two years now.\n\nAnd then someone at work will say something small, like not even directly about me, but close enough that I know they’ve heard things. No amount of papers they show me changes that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not “voices” like in a movie or something, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes houses make sounds, you know? Pipes, neighbors, cars outside—my place has this one vent that clicks at night and it drives me crazy because then you’re lying there thinking about everything you didn’t get done that day.\n\nBut other people not hearing it… I don’t know. I haven’t really gone around asking people, “Did you hear that?” Usually I’m alone when I notice noises, or I’m at work and there’s always noise—registers, scanners, somebody dropping a box in the stockroom. So… I’m not sure what exactly counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking about your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I mean, it depends what you call “one topic,” because with this whole situation everything connects, or at least it feels like it does when I’m trying to explain it.\n\nLike, if I start with the bank, then I have to explain the loan servicer, and then something that happened at work comes into it, and then someone says I’m “getting off track.” But I’m not trying to ramble. I’m trying to make sure you understand the whole picture, because if I leave out one part, people act like it doesn’t matter.\n\nSo… difficult? Maybe. I don’t really notice it unless someone points it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant stressors or life changes in the past two years, unrelated to your job or the house?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… unrelated to the job or the house is hard, because those two things sort of bled into everything else. Like, even going to the grocery store became about counting every dollar, and then you run into someone from work in the cereal aisle and suddenly it doesn’t feel separate anymore.\n\nBut if you mean like a death in the family, divorce, big move, something like that… no, I don’t think so. Nothing major that I can point to and say, “That’s the thing.” It’s mostly been… all of this. And then all the little things around it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things you see on TV, or read in the newspaper, are somehow related to you?\n\nPatient: Not like… the TV is sending me messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking the anchor is talking directly to me.\n\nBut sometimes I’ll see a story about foreclosures or banks getting fined, and it’s hard not to think, well, exactly, that’s the kind of thing I’ve been saying. Everyone keeps treating my foreclosure like it’s just paperwork and missed payments, but I know it’s not just that. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nAnd then when I try to explain it, people want to show me forms and dates and balances like that cancels out what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. It doesn’t. Papers can be made to look clean. That doesn’t mean there isn’t something going on underneath.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had similar thoughts or beliefs about other people or organizations working against you before this situation with your house?\n\nPatient: Before the house? I mean… I wouldn’t say like this, no. Not in the same way.\n\nPeople act like I’ve always been suspicious or something, but this started because of what actually happened. This is different. This has been almost two years of them quietly working together to push me out of my house, and showing me papers doesn’t make that go away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that new information could change your understanding of the situation with the bank, loan servicer, and your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess if there were real new information, sure, anyone can change their mind. Maybe in theory. But not just another clean-looking form shoved in front of me. I’m trying to make sure you understand the whole picture, because if I leave out one part, people act like it doesn’t matter.\n\nSo… difficult? Maybe. I don’t really notice it unless someone points it out.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant stressors or life changes in the past two years, unrelated to your job or the house?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… unrelated to the job or the house is hard, because those two things sort of bled into everything else. Like, even going to the grocery store became about counting every dollar, and then you run into someone from work in the cereal aisle and suddenly it doesn’t feel separate anymore.\n\nBut if you mean like a death in the family, divorce, big move, something like that… no, I don’t think so. Nothing major that I can point to and say, “That’s the thing.” It’s mostly been… all of this. Before all this with the house? No, not really… I mean, people at work can be petty, you know, especially in retail. Someone doesn’t like how you handled a customer return and suddenly your schedule looks worse, or they talk to you like you’re stupid. But that’s not the same thing.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I do think some things are related. Not every single thing, I’m not saying the whole world revolves around me or anything like that. But when something happens with my schedule at work right after I talk to the loan servicer, or someone makes a comment that sounds like they know more than they should… it’s hard to believe that’s random.

And I know people say, “Well, that’s just coincidence,” or “That’s just paperwork,” but I’ve been watching this for almost two years. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job — it’s like they’re all quietly moving in the same direction, trying to push me out of my house.

It’s funny, actually, because in retail you learn to notice patterns. Like when a customer keeps coming back with the same excuse, or when inventory numbers don’t match what’s on the shelf — you start seeing what’s really going on behind the computer screen. So when everyone keeps handing me papers like that proves I’m wrong, I just… no. The papers don’t change what I’ve been seeing.
